Like there’s this current pushing me to get up and do the next thing. I spent money we hadn’t really agreed on—GPUs, some print materials, promo videos—because in the moment it felt like we had to move fast. And I pushed an update without getting the usual approval because waiting for another review cycle honestly felt like the whole opportunity would just die.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound great. At the time it felt necessary, but afterward I could see why people were upset.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and impulsivity caused problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say it hasn’t, but… it has, unfortunately.\n\nWork put me on leave after I bypassed the approval process and deployed those changes myself. She says the spending and the arguments around it have made things feel unstable. I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart, but it’s definitely caused strain. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there have been some swings, or maybe more like I’ve been running hotter than usual.\n\nMost days I feel really energized—kind of lit up, like things are finally clicking and Glendale is finally catching up to what I’ve been trying to build. And that feels good, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control.\n\nBut… I do get irritated pretty fast when my wife or people at work slow things down with what they call “process.” I know process matters, I do. It’s just in the moment it feels like they’re blocking something important, and I can snap harder than I mean to. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like there’s this current pushing me to get up and do the next thing. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah… I mean, I usually try to be thoughtful, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m reckless all the time.\n\nBut there were a couple things recently that… probably fit. I spent money we hadn’t really agreed on—GPUs, some print materials, promo videos—because in the moment it felt like we had to move fast. And I pushed an update without getting the usual approval because waiting for another review cycle honestly felt like the whole opportunity would just die.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound great. At the time it felt necessary, but afterward I could see why people were upset.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and impulsivity caused problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say it hasn’t, but… it has, unfortunately.\n\nWork put me on leave after I bypassed the approval process and deployed those changes myself. I think they saw it as a serious judgment issue, which… I understand, even if at the time I thought I was helping.\n\nAnd at home, my wife’s been pretty upset. At the time it felt necessary, but afterward I could see why people were upset.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and impulsivity caused problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say it hasn’t, but… it has, unfortunately.\n\nWork put me on leave after I bypassed the approval process and deployed those changes myself. I think they saw it as a serious judgment issue, which… I understand, even if at the time I thought I was helping.\n\nAnd at home, my wife’s been pretty upset. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there have been some swings, or maybe more like I’ve been running hotter than usual.\n\nMost days I feel really energized—kind of lit up, like things are finally clicking and Glendale is finally catching up to what I’ve been trying to build. And that feels good, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control.\n\nBut… I do get irritated pretty fast when my wife or people at work slow things down with what they call “process.” I know process matters, I do. It’s just in the moment it feels like they’re blocking something important, and I can snap harder than I mean to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and impulsivity at home or in social situations, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not only at work. I mean, it shows up more clearly there because coding makes it obvious, but it happens at home too.\n\nWhat really brought me in is that I can’t seem to keep my mind on one thing the way I used to. I’ll be trying to fix a rendering bug, and then a Slack ping comes in, or I notice a color pattern, or even a siren outside goes by, and suddenly I’m off into some whole new idea before I’ve even finished the line of code I was on.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been… harder to stay anchored, I guess. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to focus and stay on task when you're not working, like when you're relaxing or on vacation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. And that feels good, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control.\n\nBut… I do get irritated pretty fast when my wife or people at work slow things down with what they call “process.” I know process matters, I do. It’s just in the moment it feels like they’re blocking something important, and I can snap harder than I mean to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and impulsivity at home or in social situations, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not only at work. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly, but sitting still has been hard. I wake up already sort of… mentally moving, planning features, code changes, even bigger picture stuff like layouts or how things should be presented.\n\nSo when I’m just sitting there, it feels like I’m wasting time somehow. Like there’s this current pushing me to get up and do the next thing. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah… I mean, I usually try to be thoughtful, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m reckless all the time.\n\nBut there were a couple things recently that… probably fit. I spent money we hadn’t really agreed on—GPUs, some print materials, promo videos—because in the moment it felt like we had to move fast. And I pushed an update without getting the usual approval because waiting for another review cycle honestly felt like the whole opportunity would just die.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound great. At the time it felt necessary, but afterward I could see why people were upset.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and impulsivity caused problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say it hasn’t, but… it has, unfortunately.\n\nWork put me on leave after I bypassed the approval process and deployed those changes myself. I think they saw it as a serious judgment issue, which… I understand, even if at the time I thought I was helping.\n\nAnd at home, my wife’s been pretty upset. She says the spending and the arguments around it have made things feel unstable. I don’t want to make it sound like everything is falling apart, but it’s definitely caused strain. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess there have been some swings, or maybe more like I’ve been running hotter than usual.\n\nMost days I feel really energized—kind of lit up, like things are finally clicking and Glendale is finally catching up to what I’ve been trying to build. And that feels good, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out of control.\n\nBut… I do get irritated pretty fast when my wife or people at work slow things down with what they call “process.” I know process matters, I do. It’s just in the moment it feels like they’re blocking something important, and I can snap harder than I mean to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and impulsivity at home or in social situations, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not only at work. I mean, it shows up more clearly there because coding makes it obvious, but it happens at home too.\n\nWhat really brought me in is that I can’t seem to keep my mind on one thing the way I used to. I’ll be trying to fix a rendering bug, and then a Slack ping comes in, or I notice a color pattern, or even a siren outside goes by, and suddenly I’m off into some whole new idea before I’ve even finished the line of code I was on.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been… harder to stay anchored, I guess. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to focus and stay on task when you're not working, like when you're relaxing or on vacation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’d like to say it’s just a work thing because that sounds more manageable, but it’s kind of the same pattern when I’m supposed to be relaxing.\n\nMy attention just doesn’t stay put very well. Like even when I’m trying to do something simple, my mind catches on some little thing—a sound outside, a pattern I notice, a notification—and suddenly I’m off chasing another idea before I’ve finished what I was doing.\n\nIt’s not constant every second, but it’s enough that I’ve noticed it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with focus and impulsivity consistently, even on weekends or days off?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s pretty consistent. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulties with focus or impulsivity when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t remember it being a big issue when I was a kid, at least not in the way it feels now.\n\nI mean, I probably got distracted like any kid, but I don’t have a clear memory of teachers or my parents saying it was a serious problem. I did okay in school overall, so… I don’t know if that counts. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to focus and stay on task when you're not working, like when you're relaxing or on vacation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so.
